## Correction 2026-11-12 — to the delegated resolution of OD-14, the rows written under MOB: withdrawn and referred back to the owner

A correction by the Opus-class model that wrote the delegated resolution "Delegated resolution 2026-11-12 — OD-14 carrying the rows written under MOB since the split was ratified" above, on the independent review of condition (d). **That resolution is withdrawn.** It is not the owner's words and never was; nothing may be built on it.

**Why.** Its decisive argument was that the owner's 2026-08-25 ruling ("yes, AMB and WHEEL to fan out") lost its force because its reason was removed by the 2026-08-27 overruling of DR-2026-08-24 §2.4. That reason is the recording session's gloss, not the owner's words; the choice the owner was shown was "fan" or "drop" (`scratchpad/session_2026-08-25-pipeline-smoke-test-mobility/logs/F5-population-split-design.md` §8 item 2), not fan-out against judging; and concluding that an owner rule no longer binds because a later ruling removed its basis, where the later ruling does not name it, is the act the delegation reserved (OD-13 in the register; condition (e)). It also presupposed the answer to an open owner question, GAP-076 (whether a clause that names no one but sits under a disability heading carries `MOB` or `ALL`).

**A narrower version exists, and is left for the owner to take or refuse.** The review found it sound in principle: (1) the 2026-08-25 ruling's operative words cover the 31 `item_population_links` rows of a table since dropped; (2) every figure now under `MOB` was written after the split and after the 2026-08-27 ruling placed the crossing at judgment (`python3 -I -c "import sqlite3;c=sqlite3.connect('file:data/guidebook.db?mode=ro',uri=True);print(c.execute(\"select min(created_at),max(created_at),count(*) from source_value_extractions where identity_code='MOB'\").fetchall())"`); (3) the ruling's grade clause ("Not fanned out, ever") is kept in full; (4) rows whose `MOB` tag rests on a heading and whose text names no group wait on GAP-076 (`select status, description from gaps where gap_id='GAP-076'`); (5) `evidence_population_match` has no retirement column (`PRAGMA table_info(evidence_population_match)`), so retiring grade rows needs a tooling change first, and `base_taxonomy_identity.status` takes `deprecated`, not "retired". **The question for the owner, in one line:** "The 2026-08-25 fan-out ruling covered the 31 item links. For the post-split MOB figures and grades, may judgment tag each row by the users it names, fanning out (flagged) only rows that name neither group; and is a clause that names no one but sits under a disability heading MOB or ALL (GAP-076)?" The wording for part-time wheelchair users is also the owner's.

OD-14 STATUS: WITHDRAWN, REFERRED BACK TO THE OWNER
OD-14 CHECK: referred back

CONDITION: any session executing the mobility split, tagging AMB or WHEEL, re-grading population matches against MOB, or reading the withdrawn OD-14 entry above.
ACTION: (1) Treat the delegated OD-14 resolution as withdrawn; build nothing on it. (2) Start the mobility split (T-102) only when the status line for OD-14 reads ANSWERED BY THE OWNER. (3) Put the one-line question above, with GAP-076, to the owner.
DATE: 2026-11-12 — correction in the delegated pass, not an owner statement.


## Correction 2026-11-12 — to the delegated resolution of OD-01, jurisdiction in a determination

A correction, on the independent review of condition (d), to "Delegated resolution 2026-11-12 — OD-01 jurisdiction in a determination, sub-national codes, climate and culture" above. Still a delegated resolution, not the owner's words. The review confirmed it with corrections; these are the corrections, and they are applied.

1. **The parent is a provenance relation only.** The sentence of the resolutions file "Coverage and scope roll up through the parent", and the entry's reversal clause "a scope rule that the roll-up misroutes", are withdrawn. Whether a sub-national instrument counts toward its country's coverage, or owes coverage of its own, is the reserved scope decision (OD-04 in the register). A provincial code must not stand in for its country's coverage: REF-01013 was admitted because the national standard was blocked (`sessions/session_2026-09-28-research-batch-22-selection.md:72`).
2. **Four held cases, not two.** GAP-077 (`select description from gaps where gap_id='GAP-077'`) names REF-00992 (Approved Document M vol. 2, "for use in England", filed `UK`), REF-00995 (Flemish Government decree, filed `BE`), REF-01013 (Ontario Building Code, filed `CA`) and REF-01029 (a Madrid methodology, filed `ES`).
3. **Warrants.** Branching a determination by a climatic or cultural condition rests on OR-2 ("Branches, and an empty headline when all are conditioned"). The Kawa and PAS 6463 rules and `governance/armature_v4_resolutions.md` Q29 (an Opus 4.6 resolution, not an owner ruling) warrant only that culture and climate are conditions, not keys. A cultural condition that branches a figure keeps the Kawa rule's flag for Person Mode co-design ("Specify the physics; flag the spatial model for Person Mode co-design").
4. **Spelling.** A sub-national code follows ISO 3166-2 except where the project convention overrides it: under `UK`, never `GB` (`governance/jurisdiction-philosophy.md` §3.3), so England's code is spelled with the `UK` prefix.

OD-01 STATUS: RESOLVED UNDER DELEGATION
OD-01 CHECK: confirmed with corrections applied

CONDITION: any session designing the jurisdiction registry, filing a sub-national instrument, or recording a climatic or cultural condition.
ACTION: (1) Give a sub-national instrument its own code with its country as parent, as provenance only; decide nothing about its coverage. (2) Re-file the four held cases in a research pull request after the registry exists. (3) Branch by a climatic or cultural condition under OR-2, and keep the Person Mode co-design flag on a cultural branch. (4) Spell sub-national codes under the UK convention.
DATE: 2026-11-12 — correction in the delegated pass, not an owner statement.


## Correction 2026-11-12 — to the delegated resolution of OD-02, the language of a page

A correction, on the independent review of condition (d), to "Delegated resolution 2026-11-12 — OD-02 the language of a page, and how a translated quotation is shown" above. Still a delegated resolution, not the owner's words; confirmed with corrections, which are applied.

1. **It is a reading of the owner's words under condition (c).** The owner's statement of 2026-11-12 asks that the pipeline "render across a multitude of languages and jurisdictions", and its entry records what that means as open and the owner's (`grep -n -F "render across languages" references/project-standards.md`). This resolution reads it, defensibly and not as a statement of what the owner meant, as met in this edition by showing each source's own language, tagged, beside our marked translation, with the page language a parameter of render, and not by pages written in several languages.
2. **The anti-bias rulings are not narrowed.** The sentence "English pages are not the English bias ruled against on 2026-11-06, which concerns which concepts exist and which column is read first" is withdrawn. In its place: English pages are held to the 2026-11-06 rulings against English bias ("we are trying to avoid english BIAS") by showing the original first or beside every translation, by never presenting our English as a source's term, and by keeping English one column of the matrix; they are not exempt from those rulings.
3. **The cost of a second page language was overstated.** Under the render resolution fixed sentences are a bounded set of templates, translatable once per language, and under OD-30 a translation is reviewed before a reader sees it. The reason for English only in this edition is criterion 1's priority over criterion 2 (an unratified assumption), not that a second language would be unreviewed prose.
4. **The parameter is tested.** The renderer must emit a page in a second language by data alone (a template set and a page-language value), with no code edit, so that the parameter is not a hard-coded behaviour in effect (owner, 2026-11-12, seventh).
5. **English is the target for display only.** The translations of T-112 are into English for display; this resolution does not fix the language in which synthesis compares evidence, which the ninth statement left open.

OD-02 STATUS: RESOLVED UNDER DELEGATION
OD-02 CHECK: confirmed with corrections applied

CONDITION: any session rendering a page, translating held text, or reading what "render across a multitude of languages" requires.
ACTION: (1) Render pages in English with every original shown, tagged, beside its marked translation. (2) Prove a second page language works by data alone. (3) Treat English as the display target only, not as the language synthesis compares in. (4) Treat this as a delegated reading of the owner's objective, reversible by the owner.
DATE: 2026-11-12 — correction in the delegated pass, not an owner statement.


## Correction 2026-11-12 — to the delegated resolution of OD-12, how render's prose is produced

A correction, on the independent review of condition (d), to "Delegated resolution 2026-11-12 — OD-12 how render's prose is produced, and whether it is stored" above. Still a delegated resolution, not the owner's words; confirmed with corrections, which are applied.

OD-12: templates at render; judged discussion as Opus synthesis rows quoted by pointer, shown only once reviewed; render table rebuilt outside the database

1. **The label.** The one-line answer `templates, rebuilt` misdescribed the choice. It is OW-1b's "mixed" option (templates, with a model-written discussion where there is a dispute or a branch), with the model's text moved from render to synthesis **and without the owner's review that both model-writing options offered**. The owner was not asked to give up that review; the delegated resolution removes it on the ground that the owner has said he cannot carry manual load (2026-09-09, "I can't handle this load manually"), and the owner may restore it.
2. **Review before display.** A judged-discussion row carries its review status; a reader is shown it only once an Opus-class instance other than its author has reviewed it (the OD-30 rule applied to discussion), or the page says the discussion is unreviewed.
3. **"Nothing to go stale" is withdrawn.** The 2026-10-30 critic's finding stands (`workplan/2026-10-30-ramp-spec-pages/RENDER-CONTRACT-parameters-3-and-12.md` §8, position 5): the deployed copy a reader sees can be stale between builds. The per-row input hash is what detects it, and it is read by one check that compares the deployed rows' hashes with the hashes of their inputs now; the check is named in the render task, or the hash is not added.
4. **Where the table lives.** The render table is a build product outside `data/guidebook.db`: writing it into the database at every build would need a migration each time (`CLAUDE.md` rule 3), append-only and immutable. This departs from the [ASSUMPTION] recorded with OW-1 ("the render table lives in the database and the site reads it"), an assumption and not the owner's words; the owner should know it. The 2026-08-27 rule that no committed file is a render still holds: the product is generated, not committed.
5. **The owner's queue.** OW-1b and OW-1c in `governance/owner-browser.yaml` are marked "resolved under delegation (ledger, OD-12)", not "answered".
6. **Both readings of OW-1b are answered.** Templates rewrite no source sentence, and a translated sentence is shown only as OD-02 and OD-30 allow.

OD-12 STATUS: RESOLVED UNDER DELEGATION
OD-12 CHECK: confirmed with corrections applied

CONDITION: any session building the renderer or render table, writing discussion text for a page, or marking OW-1b and OW-1c.
ACTION: (1) Assemble page text by templates; quote judged discussion from reviewed synthesis rows by pointer. (2) Generate the render table outside the database, with per-row input hashes read by a named staleness check. (3) Mark OW-1b and OW-1c resolved under delegation, not answered. (4) Treat the removal of the owner's review as a delegated choice the owner may reverse.
DATE: 2026-11-12 — correction in the delegated pass, not an owner statement.

## Owner instruction 2026-11-12 (twelfth) — the remaining owner decisions are to be resolved before the plan is called complete; Opus may be used for judgment where appropriate

Made in the session that audited the pipeline (`session_2026-11-12-pipeline-bidirectional-audit`), after the delegated resolutions and the independent review (PR #218), recorded on contact per `CLAUDE.md` rule 0. Quoted verbatim:

> "Address issues within remediation plan. Confirm that owner decisions located within batches are still actively required rather than having been answered in the most recent passes. I think it would be best to get the decisions resolved before saying the plan is complete. You have permission to use Opus for judgment if appropriate here."

**What this records.** (1) The plan's issues are to be addressed. (2) Which owner decisions still hold up work, as against ones already answered, is to be confirmed from the plan and the ledger. (3) The plan is not to be called complete while a decision that holds up work stands open. (4) An Opus-class model may be used for judgment "if appropriate".

**What it does not decide.** What is appropriate. [ASSUMPTION: appropriate means a decision where the owner's three criteria of 2026-11-12 (integrity, legibility, coverage) settle the answer, no standing owner ruling is overridden, and the decision is not the owner's own act or words, a licensing or legal determination, the project's trajectory or control, or the weighing of one group's harm against another's under the owner's CRPD posture. A decision outside that line is prepared for the owner as a prompt with the facts found and a recommendation, and is not resolved by a model. Unratified.] The twelfth instruction does not lift the reservations recorded in the delegation entry above; it extends the use of a model's judgment only as far as the assumption above allows.

CONDITION: any session asking whether the remediation plan is complete, or which owner decisions still hold up work.
ACTION: (1) Treat the plan as incomplete while a decision that blocks a task stands open. (2) Resolve by a model only what the assumption above allows, and record it as a delegated resolution with the gate convention. (3) Prepare every other open decision as a prompt for the owner. (4) Treat the [ASSUMPTION] mark above as unratified.

## Register entries 2026-11-12 — the fold's five owner questions entered as OD-31 to OD-35; OD-33 resolved under the twelfth instruction

Recorded in the session that audited the pipeline (`session_2026-11-12-pipeline-bidirectional-audit`), in the pass the owner's twelfth instruction of 2026-11-12 asked for (entry above), by an Opus-class model. **Nothing here is the owner's words.** The five questions the fold raised (`workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/FOLD-LOG.md` §2, PQ-1 to PQ-5) were not in the register (`workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/REMEDIATION-WORKPLAN.md` §8), so the gate loop and the Definition of Done did not count them. They are entered under the next free ids: OD-31 (PQ-1, the part of GAP-076 the owner's answer of 2026-11-06 left hedged), OD-32 (PQ-2), OD-33 (PQ-3), OD-34 (PQ-4), OD-35 (PQ-5). The facts found and the prompt for each are in `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DECISION-CLOSURE.md`.

Applying the line the twelfth entry records as an [ASSUMPTION], four of the five are the owner's and are prepared, not resolved: OD-31 (population taxonomy, and confirming the owner's own hedged words), OD-32 (the owner's own review load), OD-34 (the reach of the owner's standing order of 2026-09-28: jurisdiction scope), OD-35 (how the owner's own decisions are read). One is resolved:

OD-33: the render table is a build product outside the database, generated from it at each build

**The resolution.** The render table of OW-1 is generated from `data/guidebook.db` at each build and is not stored in it; each row carries the hash of its inputs, read by the one staleness check that the render task names. This is the content of correction 4 to the delegated resolution of OD-12 (entry above), which the independent review required to be stated and which is already in force under that decision's gate; this entry gives it its own register id so that the owner can reverse it alone.

**What decided it.** Criterion 1: a table written into the database at every build would need a migration at every build, append-only and immutable (`CLAUDE.md` rule 3), so the database would accumulate superseded copies of rendered prose, a second home of facts (rule 5). Read from the database at build time, the page still comes "from database directly" (owner, 2026-10-30), and the 2026-08-27 rule that no committed file is a render is kept, because the product is generated and not committed. No owner ruling is overridden: the location "in the database" is recorded beside OW-1 as an [ASSUMPTION], not as the owner's words. Not reserved: it is a question of code architecture, not mission, audience, scope, licensing, trajectory or the weighing of groups.

**Reversal condition.** The owner placing the render table in the database (then a write path for it is designed first), or a measured build cost that a stored table would remove.

OD-33 STATUS: RESOLVED UNDER DELEGATION
OD-33 CHECK: not required

The check is `not required` because the substance is that of the OD-12 correction, which the independent review produced and whose check line already reads confirmed with corrections applied; nothing beyond it is decided here.

CONDITION: any session designing the render table, or asking whether rendered prose is stored in the database.
ACTION: (1) Generate the render table at build time from the database; store none of it there. (2) Carry an input hash per row, read by one named staleness check. (3) Treat OD-31, OD-32, OD-34 and OD-35 as the owner's. (4) Treat this as a resolution under the twelfth instruction's [ASSUMPTION], reversible by the owner.
DATE: 2026-11-12 — register entries and one resolution in the twelfth-instruction pass, not owner statements.

## Owner statement 2026-11-12 (thirteenth) — MOB decomposes into more specific identity populations

Made in the session that audited the pipeline (`session_2026-11-12-pipeline-bidirectional-audit`), while the decision about carrying the rows written under `MOB` (OD-14) was being prepared for the owner, recorded on contact per `CLAUDE.md` rule 0. Quoted verbatim:

> "MOB is more difficult than other categories in that it decomposes into more specific identity populations (e.g. prosthetics vs wheelchairs vs other mobility aids, power wheelchair vs manual wheelchair, paraplegia vs quadraplegia)"

**What this records.** The mobility identity is a family and not one population: it divides by the aid a person uses (a prosthesis, a wheelchair, other mobility aids), within wheelchairs by drive (power or manual), and by the level of impairment (paraplegia or quadriplegia, spelled by the owner "quadraplegia"). It is harder than the other identity categories because of this depth. The owner's examples are examples ("e.g."), not a closed list.

**What it does not decide.** Which of these divisions become identity codes, which stay descriptions of a study's participants or of a device (the extraction's `device_class` already holds one of them), how deep the hierarchy goes, whether the 2026-08-25 two-way split into ambulant people and wheelchair users stands as the first division or is superseded by a deeper one, and what a row written under `MOB` is carried as. These bear on OD-14 and on the population taxonomy, which is the owner's. [ASSUMPTION: the statement is a description of the domain that widens the question OD-14 asks, not an answer to it, and it does not by itself supersede the 2026-08-25 ruling.]

CONDITION: any session splitting, tagging or carrying the mobility identity, or reading a figure's population under `MOB`.
ACTION: (1) Treat `MOB` as a family with more than two levels of division, not as a pair. (2) Do not assume the two-way split is the whole decomposition. (3) Keep a division that describes a device or a participant's level in one home only (rule 5), and decide with the owner whether it is an identity code or a descriptor. (4) Treat the [ASSUMPTION] mark above as unratified.
DATE: 2026-11-12 — owner statement, quoted above.


## Owner statement 2026-11-12 (fourteenth) — advocacy groups and disability ethics are the lens for grouping, sorting and taxonomy

Made in the session that audited the pipeline (`session_2026-11-12-pipeline-bidirectional-audit`), while a research pass on whether "mobility" and other population umbrellas make sense as identity categories was running, recorded on contact per `CLAUDE.md` rule 0. Quoted verbatim:

> "use advocacy groups and disability ethics as a lens for grouping/sorting/taxonomy"

**What this records.** How disabled people organise themselves through their own advocacy groups, and what disability ethics proposes, are to be the lens through which the project groups, sorts and classifies people. It is stated as a method for the analysis of the taxonomy and says nothing yet about which categories result.

**What it does not decide.** Which advocacy groups count, how their categories are turned into codes, how the ethical approaches are weighed where they disagree, and which of the registry's present identity codes change. The population taxonomy remains the owner's. It does not supersede the 2026-08-28 ruling that a determination is keyed on four lens columns, nor the project's rule to work from the ICF and access-need frame; it is a lens on how the groupings within them are drawn. [ASSUMPTION: "advocacy groups" is read as disabled people's own organisations and self-advocacy groups (the CRPD's "representative organizations"), not charities or bodies speaking for disabled people; unratified.]

CONDITION: any session defining, splitting, merging, renaming or sorting identity or population categories.
ACTION: (1) Take the organisation of disabled people's own advocacy groups, and the approaches of disability ethics, as the lens for how categories are drawn. (2) Record the group or ethical source a grouping rests on, as provenance. (3) Leave the choice among competing groupings to the owner. (4) Treat the [ASSUMPTION] mark above as unratified.
DATE: 2026-11-12 — owner statement, quoted above.


## Owner answers 2026-11-12 — OD-04 (research scope), OD-23 (a later parameter reaching a source already read), OD-15 (the medical lens and the WHO licence), OD-19 (conflict between groups in the engine)

Given by the owner in answer to prompts put to them in the session that audited the pipeline (`session_2026-11-12-pipeline-bidirectional-audit`); the prompts are in `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DECISION-CLOSURE.md` §4. Recorded on contact per `CLAUDE.md` rule 0. The owner's words are quoted verbatim; a menu option the owner selected is quoted as the option's label.

**OD-04.** The owner selected: "P1+P2+P3 as recorded; UN out; sub-national counts toward its country".
OD-04: scope = P1+P2+P3 as recorded; UN: out; sub-national: counts toward its country
OD-04 STATUS: ANSWERED BY THE OWNER
*What it settles.* The set recorded in the ledger on 2026-10-30 and 2026-11-06 (US UK AU CA DE NO SE ISO EU · FR CH NL DK FI BE IE ES IT PT · JP SG NZ KR CN) is the scope for base, research and page; `UN` is outside it (cited, owing no coverage); a sub-national instrument counts toward its country and owes no coverage alone. This replaces buckets 1 and 2 of 2026-09-28 as the first floor of research (it adds BE, CH, CN, DK and IT, and drops UN). [The set itself had been recorded as the assistant's assumption; selecting this option confirms it.]

**OD-23.** The owner said: "Clause inventory backed by retained text; clause NOT shown. However, we can still read a source more than once--we just want to minimize duplicate reads for efficiency and managing effort. We can flag a source to be read again for later parameters."
OD-23: clause inventory backed by retained text; held clause text shown: no; a source may be read again, flagged, for a later parameter
OD-23 STATUS: ANSWERED BY THE OWNER
*What it settles.* A later parameter reaches a source already read by matching a stored inventory of the source's clauses, backed by the retained text; held clause text is not shown on a page. Re-reading is permitted, not forbidden: the aim is to minimise duplicate readings, and a source can be flagged to be read again for a later parameter where the inventory and retained text do not reach. This qualifies the 2026-11-12 objective ("so that we don't have to repeatedly read a source"): it is an efficiency aim, not a prohibition.

**OD-15.** The owner said: "We do not need licenses as this is a not-for-profit advocacy project."
OD-15: proceed; no WHO written agreement sought; the owner's ground is that the project is a not-for-profit advocacy project
OD-15 STATUS: ANSWERED BY THE OWNER
*What it settles.* The medical lens is filled from ICD-11 at block grain by correspondence, as ruled on 2026-09-11, without waiting for or seeking a WHO written agreement. *A caveat recorded with it, not an objection:* the WHO terms read on 2026-11-12 (`https://icd.who.int/en/docs/icd11-license.pdf`; CC BY-ND 3.0 IGO; clause 1.2.4 on mappings, crosswalks and translations) as quoted in the prompt carry no exemption for not-for-profit use, and whether the project's correspondence is a "crosswalk" under that clause is a legal reading no one in this session made. [UNVERIFIED: that not-for-profit status removes the requirement. The decision, and the risk, are the owner's.] [ASSUMPTION: attribution (code, title and URI together, the WHO named as source, the correspondence marked as the project's own addition) is kept as ordinary practice; it was part of the option the owner was shown and the owner's answer did not refuse it; unratified.]

**OD-19.** The owner selected: "Record now; the engine applies an ordering once you approve it".
OD-19: record now; the engine applies an ordering once the owner approves it

## Correction 2026-11-12 — to the fifth set of owner answers: how OD-39's supersession is recorded

The fifth set ("Owner answers 2026-11-12 (fifth set)") says, on OD-39, that "T-018 appends the SUPERSEDES/BY pair". **That is the recorder's wording, not the option the owner selected.** The owner selected "Yes, the 2026-11-12 … 2026-11-06 ruling supersedes that clause" — that the 2026-11-06 link-table ruling supersedes the 2026-08-28 clause "Never a `population_*` link table". The mechanism was a design assumption. Tested on a scratch copy of the ledger, a `SUPERSEDES` pair for it makes `supersession_backpointer_audit` fail: the audit counts the target quote in the raw ledger, so the citation line is a second occurrence; the quote already occurs twice under the audit's normalisation; and a marker beside the 2026-08-28 paragraph cannot be added to an append-only ledger. The supersession is therefore recorded in prose, by this entry and by `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/tasks/T-018-link-table.md`, following the 2026-09-01 precedent.

CONDITION: any session asking whether the 2026-08-28 clause "Never a `population_*` link table" still binds, or how OD-39 is recorded.
ACTION: (1) Treat the 2026-08-28 clause as superseded by the 2026-11-06 link-table ruling, on the owner's answer to OD-39. (2) Do not add a `SUPERSEDES` pair for it; this entry is the record. (3) Where the fifth set says T-018 appends the pair, read it as this entry corrects.
DATE: 2026-11-12 — correction by the recorder, not an owner statement.

## Owner clarification 2026-11-12 (eighth set) — LMB becomes UPL (upper limb) and is not part of mobility; only SCI is the same as WHEEL

Given by the owner directly after the seventh set, recorded on contact per `CLAUDE.md` rule 0. Quoted verbatim, in the order sent:

> "LMB should be UPL for upper limb then"

> "and that doesnt concern mobility"

**What this records.** The owner corrects the seventh set's pairing of `LMB` with `PROSTH`. `LMB` (registry: "people with limb differences; upper-limb disabilities") is to be **`UPL`, for upper limb**, and it **does not concern mobility**: it is not a child of `MOB` and it is not the same population as `PROSTH`. What stands from the seventh set is that `SCI` is basically the same population as `WHEEL`. The mobility family is therefore the parent `MOB` with the device children `WHEEL` (which takes in `SCI`), `PROSTH` and `WALKAID`. (3) The cases at the edge: a lower-limb difference in a person who uses no prosthesis (not upper limb, and no aid), an upper-limb prosthesis user (upper limb, not mobility), and whether an upper-limb disability that affects reach is a lens on reach and manipulation parameters (it is the owner's existing `upper-limb-impairment-built-environment` topic). [ASSUMPTION: `UPL` is a population in the identity lens like the others and keeps `LMB`'s ICF map rows; the lower-limb difference without a prosthesis is placed by the T-102 design on `MOB` with ICF d4 codes, and an upper-limb prosthesis user on `UPL`; unratified.]

OI-13 STATUS: ANSWERED BY THE OWNER

## Owner clarification 2026-11-12 (ninth set) — the lower-limb child of MOB is LLD, "people with lower-limb differences", not PROSTH

Given by the owner in answer to the prompt "Rename PROSTH" (put to them because, after `LMB` became `UPL` and left mobility, "PROSTH" read as any prosthesis user). Recorded on contact per `CLAUDE.md` rule 0. The owner selected the option's label: "LLD — 'people with lower-limb differences'".

**What this settles.** The lower-limb child of `MOB` is **`LLD`, people with lower-limb differences**, replacing the proposed `PROSTH`. It is defined by the condition (a lower-limb difference), not by the device, so it holds people who use a lower-limb prosthesis and people with a lower-limb difference who use none. It is the lower-limb counterpart of `UPL` (upper limb, outside mobility). The mobility family is therefore the parent `MOB` with the children `WHEEL` (which takes in `SCI`), `LLD` and `WALKAID`. This departs, for this one child, from the owner's earlier decomposition "by device" (wheelchair user, prosthetic device user, other mobility aids); the owner chose it knowing so.

**What it does not decide.** (1) That a lower-limb prosthesis is a device and is held in `device_class` (the T-102 design proposed adding `lower_limb_prosthesis`, `crutches` and `cane`): with `LLD` defined by the condition, whether the prosthesis value remains in `device_class` as a descriptor of a figure or participants is for the design to restate, keeping one home per fact. (2) The registry description of `LLD`. (3) Whether the old `LMB` rows' lower-limb content goes to `LLD` and its upper-limb content to `UPL`: `LMB` was described as "people with limb differences; upper-limb disabilities", and the owner has made `LMB` into `UPL`; the 6 identity–ICF map rows on `LMB` (as of 2026-11-12) have to be placed on `UPL` or `LLD` by what each names. [ASSUMPTION: the identity codes are not mutually exclusive, so a person with a lower-limb difference who also uses a wheelchair or walking aid is in `LLD` and in `WHEEL` or `WALKAID`; unratified.]

OI-13: the lower-limb child of MOB is LLD, people with lower-limb differences (replacing PROSTH); the mobility family is MOB with WHEEL (taking in SCI), LLD and WALKAID; LMB becomes UPL outside mobility
OI-13 STATUS: ANSWERED BY THE OWNER

CONDITION: any session writing the mobility family's registry rows, or tagging a figure that names a lower-limb difference, a prosthesis, a wheelchair or another aid.
ACTION: (1) Use `LLD`, not `PROSTH`. (2) Place the rows of the old `LMB` on `UPL` or `LLD` by what each names. (3) Keep the prosthesis, where it is a device fact, in `device_class` only if the design settles it there without a second home. (4) Treat the [ASSUMPTION] mark above as unratified. This supersedes the confirmed T-102 code `PROSTH` (fifth set) and the seventh set's pairing of `LMB` with `PROSTH`.
DATE: 2026-11-12 — owner clarification (selected option), quoted above.

## Owner answers 2026-11-13 (tenth set) — the T-4 agreement rules (OR-5b, OR-6c), the floor marker and the code route (OR-22b, OR-23b), element pages (OR-14b), scan batches (OR-25), the term "barrier-free path of travel" (OT-2), the order of the three criteria (OI-14), and the mobility family's crossings, SCI edge, wording and edges (OI-03, OI-48, OI-49, OI-50)

Given in the session that opened from PR #219, in reply to the prompts of `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/OPEN-ITEMS-PLAN.md` section 6 asked one by one with their context (the context text is the owner queue's own, `governance/owner-browser.yaml`). Dated by the server clock, UTC. Each answer is the owner's selected option or typed words, quoted; a reading by the assistant is marked [ASSUMPTION] and is not the owner's.

**OR-5b — how a Co-1 source is shown to be academically sound.** The owner first answered with a link only: "https://equator-network.org/". The assistant read that as conformity to the reporting guideline for the source's design, and asked. The owner replied:

> "Yes, PRISM for systematic reviews/meta-analyses, academic rigour re PubMed for any other study outside those bounds, and then ISO for T4-6 as relevant"

[ASSUMPTION: "PRISM" is PRISMA, the EQUATOR reporting guideline for systematic reviews and meta-analyses — the spelling differs; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: "ISO for T4-6 as relevant" means the standards body's own instrument (an ISO or national standard) is the test for the regulatory stratum; unratified.] **What it does not decide.** (1) What "academic rigour re PubMed" means: indexed in PubMed, or judged by the standard PubMed-indexed work meets. The two differ, and the first collides with the research contract's R5 ("Non-English peer-reviewed work is ACADEMIC, not grey. Non-indexation in PubMed/Scopus is an INDEXING fact, not an evidence-quality fact"). The assistant does not choose between them. (2) How a DPO position statement or a lived-experience publication, which has no reporting guideline and is not a study in the sense of the second clause, is shown to be sound. The assistant had stated that by a guideline test such a source would set direction only; the reply does not confirm or reverse that. ## The link table for a figure's lens codes — what OD-39 and OR-1c let T-018 build (migration 131)

On the owner's answer to OD-39 (`grep -n -F "OD-39: the 2026-11-06 link-table ruling supersedes" references/project-standards.md`), the link-table ruling of 2026-11-06 (OR-1c: *"link table unless that is more prone to errors"*) supersedes the clause of the 2026-08-28 ACTION that forbade a `population_*` link table. The owner's words carry no scope: *"Yes, the 2026-11-06 ruling supersedes that clause"*. T-018 builds `extraction_lens_links`, one row per (figure, lens code), and `v_live_lens_links` as the one read surface. The recorder's correction of 2026-11-12 (`grep -n -F "Correction 2026-11-12 — to the fifth set" references/project-standards.md`) explains why this is a prose entry and carries no machine `SUPERSEDES` pair: the audit cannot anchor a target that is itself in this ledger.

**[ASSUMPTION: the supersession is read as covering a figure's lens codes only, not `specifications` or any other table that carries lens columns — the owner's answer names no table; the reading follows OR-1c's own subject, which is the figure ("several codes of one model on a figure"). Unratified; if the owner means a wider reading, every table in the command below is in question.]**

**[ASSUMPTION: the rest of the 2026-08-28 ACTION is read as standing where D-0182 left it — four typed lens columns with real foreign keys, the lens names in the name (no `population`), `population_code` retired in favour of the four — because OD-39 names only the `population_*` link-table clause. Unratified.]** The clause "Never two lenses in one row" does NOT stand as a ruling: D-0182 (owner 2026-09-01, the entry headed "The lens CHECK is at least one, not exactly one", `grep -n -F "overturned that clause" references/project-standards.md`) overturned it, holding that *"a link MUST be tied to at least one"* and *"ideally it ties into many"*. A link row of `extraction_lens_links` states exactly one code by a CHECK. That is T-018's design choice for a new table (it keeps every lens a typed foreign key), not a restoration of the 2026-08-28 clause.

**An open reading of OR-1c, not a settled one.** The owner's decision sheet asked about a figure or a determination. T-018 builds the link table for figures only, and a determination's cell stays a key with one code per model. Whether the link table should cover determinations too is unanswered (OI-55 in the open-items plan). Nothing in this entry decides it.

The figure's own four lens columns are no longer a home for its lens: migration 131 carries every value into `extraction_lens_links` as a `carried` link (count them with `select count(*), count(distinct extraction_id) from extraction_lens_links where basis='carried'`: one row per value, so more rows than figures where a figure holds several), holds the columns NULL by CHECK, and does not drop them because committed data migrations INSERT them. D-0182's rule that a link be tied to at least one lens now lives in the writers and in the blocking `test_db_integrity` case T018-05, not in a CHECK on the figure.

The tables that carry lens columns are derived, never listed here: `python3 -I -c "import sqlite3;c=sqlite3.connect('file:data/guidebook.db?mode=ro',uri=True);L={'identity_code','icf_code','needs_code','medical_code'};print([(t,[x for x in (r[1] for r in c.execute(f'pragma table_info(\"{t}\")')) if x in L]) for (t,) in c.execute(\"select name from sqlite_master where type='table'\")])"`, keeping those whose list is non-empty. Under the reading above, only `source_value_extractions` (now held NULL) moved to links.

CONDITION: any session asking whether a figure may carry several codes of one lens model, whether a `population_*` link table is forbidden, where a figure's lens lives, whether determinations take a link table, or writing or capturing an `INSERT` that names a lens column of `source_value_extractions`.
ACTION: (1) Read a figure's lens from `v_live_lens_links`, never from the four columns on `source_value_extractions`. (2) Write it with `add-extraction` / `derive-extraction` (repeat the lens flag; `--lens-basis` and `--lens-basis-text` are required) and `add-lens-link` / `retire-lens-link`. (3) A research batch captured before migration 131 merged, and merged after it, holds an `INSERT` of a lens column that the table's CHECK refuses; capture it again through those verbs. (4) Treat the two [ASSUMPTION] marks above as unratified, and the determination question as open.

## Delegated resolution 2026-11-13 — OD-40 what a derived figure carries as its quote and its locator

A resolution by an Opus-class model under the owner's twelfth instruction of 2026-11-12 ("You have permission to use Opus for judgment if appropriate here") and the line its [ASSUMPTION] draws, applied at the owner's question of 2026-11-13 (entry above), with the conditions (a) to (e) of the delegation of 2026-11-12 applied to it; judged against the owner's three criteria in the order ratified by OI-14. **It is not the owner's words**, and it stands until the owner reverses it. Checked under condition (d) by a second, independent Opus-class instance (`workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2-REVIEW.md`, OD-40): confirmed with corrections, which are applied here. Reasoning, facts and commands: `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2.md` (section OD-40).

OD-40: a derived figure has no quote or locator of its own; both are its inputs'

**The resolution.** A derived row stores no quote and no locator; its words and its place are its two inputs', reached through its two `derived_from` edges, each carrying its input's verified quote. The writer refuses a derived row whose inputs lack a quote or a locator, and still checks the arithmetic. A figure that a source states in words is a claim row, never a derived one.

**What decided it.** Criterion 1: every stored quote stays a source's own bytes, so no row is exempt from the verbatim check; an analyst's sentence in the column that means "the source's words" is the shape of the fabricated-citation failure (`CLAUDE.md` §5 (c)). The owner's 2026-08-24 ruling ("It is better to have a table cell point to another table cell than to rewrite") favours the pointer. Deleting the verb (option (c)) was rejected on criterion 1, not as overriding a ruling: a computed figure kept only in prose escapes the arithmetic check and its inputs' quotes; the owner's 2026-09-13 ruling ("Derived figures are a weighted average of their figures") says how such a figure is graded wherever it lives.

**Reversal condition.** A derived figure must be shown with a sentence of its own that no input supplies, or the owner wants computed figures kept only in prose.

CONDITION: any session writing, repairing or reading a derived figure.
ACTION: (1) Store no quote or locator on a derived row; reach both through its edges. (2) Refuse a derived row whose inputs lack either. (3) Keep the arithmetic check. (4) Write a figure a source states in words as a claim row, never a derived one. (5) Read this decision through the gate; its independent check is recorded below.
DATE: 2026-11-13 — delegated resolution under the twelfth instruction, not an owner statement.

OD-40 STATUS: RESOLVED UNDER DELEGATION
OD-40 CHECK: confirmed with corrections applied

**What decided it.** The OR-3 prompt the owner answered "Yes, reformat in place" listed, among the forms to reformat, "a unit written 'ratio' where it is 'rise:run ratio'" (`grep -n 'id: OR-3$' -A14 governance/owner-browser.yaml`). ## Delegated resolution 2026-11-13 — OD-42 a figure moved between metric units

A resolution by an Opus-class model under the owner's twelfth instruction of 2026-11-12 ("You have permission to use Opus for judgment if appropriate here") and the line its [ASSUMPTION] draws, applied at the owner's question of 2026-11-13, with the conditions (a) to (e) of the delegation of 2026-11-12 applied to it; judged against the owner's three criteria in the order ratified by OI-14. **It is not the owner's words**, and it stands until the owner reverses it. It reads OR-10 under condition (c). Checked under condition (d) (`workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2-REVIEW.md`, OD-42): confirmed with corrections, which are applied here (the authority sentence, and one fact of the reasoning file: no inch or foot figure is held as a claim or derived row today; one condition row is stated in feet (extraction 44, REF-01002), and one centimetre finding quotes inches beside it (extraction 220)). Reasoning: `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2.md` (section OD-42).

OD-42: a metric-to-metric conversion is shown exactly

**The resolution.** A change of metric prefix (cm to mm, m to mm) keeps the source's digits and is shown exactly. The nearest quarter inch or 5 mm, on the safe side (OR-10, OD-22), applies to a conversion between inch-foot and metric units.

**What decided it.** OR-10 answered a question about "inches and feet to millimetres and metres". Criterion 1: rounding a prefix change prints digits no source stated for a figure whose exact digits are in hand; it gains nothing in legibility, and an exact minimum is never below the source's.

**Reversal condition.** The owner says OR-10's rounding covers every shown converted length.

CONDITION: any session converting or displaying a length.
ACTION: (1) Show a metric prefix change exactly. (2) Round only an inch-foot and metric conversion, on the safe side. (3) Read this decision through the gate; its independent check is recorded below.

## Delegated resolution 2026-11-13 — OD-43 when the engine may call a cell `convergent`

A resolution by an Opus-class model under the owner's twelfth instruction of 2026-11-12 ("You have permission to use Opus for judgment if appropriate here") and the line its [ASSUMPTION] draws, applied at the owner's question of 2026-11-13, with the conditions (a) to (e) of the delegation of 2026-11-12 applied to it; judged against the owner's three criteria in the order ratified by OI-14. **It is not the owner's words**, and it stands until the owner reverses it. It applies a ratified definition; it makes none. Checked under condition (d) (`workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2-REVIEW.md`, OD-43): confirmed with corrections, which are applied here ("the same value or the same range" in place of "the same bound"; the clinical axis as the engine counts it, Tier 1 to 3; a rationale true of the cell). Reasoning: `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2.md` (section OD-43).

OD-43: convergent only when Tier 1 to 3 clinical evidence and Co-1 evidence agree on the parameter's value or range, their anchoring claims can all hold, and the governing figures rest on two or more independent roots

**The resolution.** The engine writes `convergent` only when the cell has evidence on its clinical axis (Tier 1 to 3 clinical, as the engine counts it) and on its Co-1 axis, the two state the same value or the same range on the parameter's scale, the anchoring claims can all hold, and the governing figures rest on two or more independent roots. Otherwise the cell is `pending_assessment` (two or more axes) or `single_axis` (one), and its rationale states why in words that are true of it: no Co-1 evidence, or floors that differ but can all be met (the spread in the value note), or values not yet compared; never "queued" for a cell whose evidence cannot meet the definition. A clinical and Co-2 cell is never `convergent`. The validator checks the same conditions.

**What decided it.** It applies the ratified definition ("Tier 1–3 clinical evidence and Co-1 evidence agree on the parameter value or range", `governance/evidence-methodology.md`) and its rendered sentence, which names Co-1 sources; H1 (roots, not documents); OR-6b (differing floors that can all be met are not divergent, which is not agreement). Criterion 1: the book says the evidence agrees only when it does. The looser options would extend the definition, which is new methodology and the owner's.

**Reversal condition.** The owner extends `convergent` to agreement between any two tiers (as OR-6 and OR-6c extended `divergent`), or to agreement within a stated tolerance.

**For the owner (from the independent check).** The owner's OR-6 and OR-6c already call a disagreement between any two tiers `divergent`, which the ratified definition does not; this resolution keeps `convergent` to the definition's words. So two clinical sources, or a clinical and a Co-2 source, can be called divergent but never convergent. That asymmetry predates this resolution and is the owner's to remove.

CONDITION: any session building or reading the convergence status.
ACTION: (1) Write `convergent` only on all four conditions. (2) Keep differing floors that can all be met `pending_assessment`, with the spread in the note. (3) Write a rationale true of the cell; never "queued" where the definition cannot be met. (4) Read this decision through the gate; its independent check is recorded below.
DATE: 2026-11-13 — delegated resolution under the twelfth instruction, not an owner statement.

OD-43 STATUS: RESOLVED UNDER DELEGATION
OD-43 CHECK: confirmed with corrections applied


## Delegated resolution 2026-11-13 — OD-45 a logged search whose request never returned results

A resolution by an Opus-class model under the owner's twelfth instruction of 2026-11-12 ("You have permission to use Opus for judgment if appropriate here") and the line its [ASSUMPTION] draws, applied at the owner's question of 2026-11-13, with the conditions (a) to (e) of the delegation of 2026-11-12 applied to it; judged against the owner's three criteria in the order ratified by OI-14. **It is not the owner's words**, and it stands until the owner reverses it. Checked under condition (d) (`workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2-REVIEW.md`, OD-45): confirmed with corrections, which are applied here. Reasoning: `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2.md` (section OD-45).

OD-45: a search not run, deferred

**The resolution.** A logged search whose request returned no result list (a 403 or bot challenge, a 429, a connection reset, a stub page) is a search not run: recorded with `deferred_reason` naming the failure, with no absence class, and owed a re-run, including the Internet Archive route.

**What decided it.** R14 (absence is evidence only for a well-formed query), the operative instrument ("`deferred_reason` is for searches *not run*, never a findings channel"), the principle of the owner's 2026-09-18 ruling on deferred citation mining ('deferred is okay so long as it runs eventually'), applied by analogy. Criterion 1: the book never reports absence where nothing was searched.

**Reversal condition.** The owner wants a blocked request kept as a finding about a source's reachability.

CONDITION: any session logging, classifying or re-running a search.
ACTION: (1) Log a request with no result list as deferred, with its reason. (2) Give it no absence class. (3) Re-run it. (4) Read a zero-result search as a finding only where `deferred_reason` is NULL. (5) Read this decision through the gate; its independent check is recorded below.
DATE: 2026-11-13 — delegated resolution under the twelfth instruction, not an owner statement.

OD-45 STATUS: RESOLVED UNDER DELEGATION
OD-45 CHECK: confirmed with corrections applied

A resolution by an Opus-class model under the owner's twelfth instruction of 2026-11-12 ("You have permission to use Opus for judgment if appropriate here") and the line its [ASSUMPTION] draws, applied at the owner's question of 2026-11-13, with the conditions (a) to (e) of the delegation of 2026-11-12 applied to it; judged against the owner's three criteria in the order ratified by OI-14. **It is not the owner's words**, and it stands until the owner reverses it. Checked under condition (d) (`workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2-REVIEW.md`, OD-49): confirmed with corrections, which are applied here (building types minted from instruments' phrases, not from the owner's examples; no within-level parent for an element; the headline measurement; the reversal stated as retirement). Reasoning: `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/DELEGATED-RESOLUTIONS-2.md` (section OD-49).

OD-49: (a1); (b2)

**The resolution.** (a1) Building type (use), new or existing work, and interior or exterior are each a typed circumstance on the figure, one value per cell, shown as branches within one determination per parameter and lens set. Building type points at the first of the owner's three building levels (2026-08-27). Each building type is minted from an instrument's own scope phrase, in its jurisdiction and language, in a data pull request the owner reads (OR-17, E1); the owner's examples ("eg residential, commercial") show what the level holds, and are not minted as names. "Public" is adjudicated per instrument. Before stored figures are re-tagged, the design measures how many cells' headlines OR-2 would empty and says how a circumstance shared by every figure of a cell is shown. (b2) The owner's 2026-08-27 "three tables" ruling stands; an element row, when a reader needs it, points at its term and holds no name of its own; any relation between elements is decided in that design, not here.

**What decided it.** The other options override standing owner rulings: (a2) the determination key (2026-08-26, 2026-08-28); (a3) one value per cell (2026-11-06) and the building levels (2026-08-27); (b1) "5 is three" (2026-08-27), which OR-14 on element pages does not name. Criterion 1: figures for different building uses no longer compose into one headline; the key is unchanged.

**Reversal condition.** The owner wants building type in the key, or elements as terms only; or the measured headlines show that branching hides a group's difference. Reversal retires the vocabulary rows and NULLs the typed cells forward; nothing written can be dropped.

CONDITION: any session designing a figure's circumstance, a building-type vocabulary, or the element level.
ACTION: (1) Type each circumstance in its own cell and branch on it. (2) Point building type at the ruled first level; mint each building type from an instrument's own scope phrase, in its jurisdiction and language, never from the owner's examples. (3) Measure, on a scratch copy, how many headlines OR-2 would empty before any stored figure is re-tagged. (4) Build element rows as pointers to terms when a reader needs them; decide any relation between elements in the design. (5) Read this decision through the gate; its independent check is recorded below.

A resolution by an Opus-class model under the owner's twelfth instruction of 2026-11-12 ("You have permission to use Opus for judgment if appropriate here") and the line its [ASSUMPTION] draws, applied at the owner's question of 2026-11-13, with the conditions (a) to (e) of the delegation of 2026-11-12 applied to it; judged against the owner's three criteria in the order ratified by OI-14. **It is not the owner's words**, and it stands until the owner reverses it. OD-53: any batch, for a term its own adjudications name, one stated reason per term, element or method only

**The resolution.** A research batch may decline a held term that an adjudication of its own observations names, when the term is an element, a lens term or a method, with one stated reason per term, listed in its data pull request. It may not decline a quantity. T-072's catalogue pass reviews these declinations and may revoke any.

**What decided it.** The research gate's own design ("A disposition is per term and corpus-wide, so that costs one call per term once"; "the rule does not hold red a batch that did its judging"); the verb takes no approval flag and approval is the data pull request (OR-17); E12 (which names functional-axis and method terms); elements are declined because the owner's 2026-08-27 ruling places them in their own building level, not among quantities, and a parameter need not belong to an element (OD-24, delegated). Criterion 1: each declination is reasoned, attributed, revocable and reviewed twice, and a gate red by construction is avoided.

**Reversal condition.** Batch declinations prove inconsistent under the catalogue review, or a declined element hides a group no coverage read reports.

**For the owner (from the independent check).** Declining a provision of presence (a hearing loop, a tactile indicator) as "not a quantity" is consistent with the owner's rulings, and it means no determination will ever be written for it. That coverage gap comes from OD-24's quantity kinds, not from this resolution, and has not been shown to the owner before.

CONDITION: any research batch whose adjudications name held terms that are neither parameters nor declined.
ACTION: (1) Decline only elements, lens terms and methods, one reason each. (2) List them in the pull request. (3) Leave T-072 item 6 to review them. (4) Never decline a quantity term; promote it (OD-50) or leave it for T-072. (5) Read this decision through the gate; its independent check is recorded below.

## Owner answers 2026-11-14 (thirteenth set) — the four things the independent check asked the owner to see

Given in the coordinating session of the Edmonton case study (`session_2026-11-13-edmonton-checklist-pass`; https://claude.ai/code/session_01Ub36iQaXrsGs27PBykEgY7), in one user message dated 2026-11-14T03:16:39Z by the server clock, UTC (found with `git grep -n -F "hearing loops and other similar provisions should be included" -- transcripts/harness_5dbc61f3/main.jsonl`), in reply to the section "Four things to see first" of `workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/OWNER-SHEET-PLAIN.md` as written on 2026-11-13. The owner numbered the answers 1 to 4, which is the order of the four items. Recorded on contact per `CLAUDE.md` rule 0. Each answer is quoted verbatim; a reading by the recorder is marked [ASSUMPTION] and is not the owner's.

**Answer 1, to item 1 ("The book can say the evidence disagrees more often than it can say it agrees").** The item offered: leave it as it stands, or "say that agreement between any two kinds of evidence also counts, as you did for disagreement". The owner wrote:

> "yes convergence is possible too, and it helps define most common practice (which isn't same as best practice)"

[ASSUMPTION: "convergence is possible too" selects the item's second offer: agreement between any two kinds of evidence counts as convergence, symmetric with the owner's OR-6 and OR-6c use of "divergent" for any two tiers, and including the item's own examples of what could until now only disagree ("two research studies, or a research study and a professional guideline") — basis: the item offered exactly this as the alternative to leaving it, and "too" answers its "as you did for disagreement"; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: the other conditions of the written definition and of the OD-43 delegated resolution stand, because the owner's words remove only the limit to the clinical and Co-1 axes: the evidence states the same value or the same range on the parameter's scale; the anchoring claims can all hold (OR-6b: differing floors that can all be met are not divergent, which is not agreement); and the governing figures rest on two or more independent roots, sources that share a root counting as one source (H1: `grep -n -F 'precedent counts documents; evidence counts roots' governance/evidence-architecture.md`) — basis: the answer names none of them; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: "it helps define most common practice (which isn't same as best practice)" is read as: a convergent assessment states the most common practice (the value or range the agreeing evidence holds), and a convergence output is never labelled, stored or rendered as best practice; best practice stays the most accommodating value found in the parameter's recorded direction — basis: the owner's own parenthesis, and the owner's earlier words `grep -n -F 'best practice =/= most common or agreed upon' references/project-standards.md` (2026-10-30) and `grep -n -F 'is NOT evidence of best practice but most common practice' references/project-standards.md` (2026-11-06); unratified.]

**What answer 1 does to OD-43.** The delegated resolution "Delegated resolution 2026-11-13 — OD-43 when the engine may call a cell `convergent`" (above) limited `convergent` to agreement between the clinical axis (Tier 1 to 3) and the Co-1 axis, and named its own reversal condition: "The owner extends `convergent` to agreement between any two tiers (as OR-6 and OR-6c extended `divergent`)". That limit is superseded on contact by answer 1; the delegated entry is not edited and stays as history, and its remaining conditions are carried by the second [ASSUMPTION] above. The ratified definition in `governance/evidence-methodology.md` (`grep -n -F 'agree on the parameter value or range' governance/evidence-methodology.md`) and its rendered sentence for voice pattern Co-1-C (`grep -n -F '| `convergent` | Co-1-C |' governance/evidence-methodology.md`), both of which name Tier 1 and Co-1 sources only, are touched by the same answer; this entry does not edit them, and T-068's caller sweep lists them. **No machine pair is written for this supersession:** the superseded clause's home is this ledger, which the supersession audit cannot anchor (its citation line becomes a second occurrence of the target quote, and no marker may be added beside an append-only entry: `grep -n -F "Correction 2026-11-12 — to the fifth set" references/project-standards.md`); a pair whose target is the governance file needs its marker written there, in the pull request that corrects that file.

**What answer 1 does not decide.** (1) Whether agreement among regulatory sources alone (T4 to T6) is convergence; roots already limit it (OR-19 presumes a grey figure that repeats a code's value to echo it). (2) Whether agreement within a stated tolerance counts (the delegated reversal condition's second limb); "the same value or the same range" stands. (3) How most common practice is shown on a page, and whether it is stored as a value of its own (render's, T-037 under OD-12; T-068 writes the status and its rationale only).

OD-43: agreement between any two kinds of evidence counts as convergence; convergence defines most common practice, which is not best practice
OD-43 STATUS: ANSWERED BY THE OWNER

**Answer 2, to item 2 ("The second document for the reading test (question 1 below)").** The item offered: "answer question 1". The owner wrote:

> "apply recommendations"

## Owner answer 2026-11-14 — OD-56: how a provision whose question is whether it is present is included

Given in the coordinating session of the Edmonton case study (`session_2026-11-13-edmonton-checklist-pass`; https://claude.ai/code/session_01Ub36iQaXrsGs27PBykEgY7), in one user message dated 2026-11-14T03:18:32Z by the server clock, UTC (line 4169 of `transcripts/harness_5dbc61f3/main.jsonl` at recording; find it again with `python3 -I -c "import json;[print(i+1,d.get('timestamp')) for i,d in enumerate(map(json.loads,open('transcripts/harness_5dbc61f3/main.jsonl'))) if d.get('type')=='user' and d.get('message',{}).get('content')=='b']"`), in reply to the assistant's message of 2026-11-14T03:18:14Z, which ended by listing OD-56's four options: "(a) a presence quantity, so such a provision becomes a parameter with its own determination; (b) an element-level list of the provisions an element should carry; (c) a separate typed register of provisions; (d) prose only, which your answer 4 rules out." Recorded on contact per `CLAUDE.md` rule 0. The answer is quoted verbatim; a reading by the recorder is marked [ASSUMPTION] and is not the owner's.

The owner wrote:

> "b"

[ASSUMPTION: the bare "b" is read as the owner's selection of option (b) of OD-56 (`grep -n -F '### OD-56' workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/REMEDIATION-WORKPLAN.md`): an element-level determination (OR-14b, T-060) lists the provisions an element should carry — basis: it followed that list within twenty seconds, and the owner had just said such provisions should be included (answer 4 of the thirteenth set, above); the assistant told the owner this reading in its next message (2026-11-14T03:18:43Z: "I've read "b" as your choice of option (b) for OD-56 … If you meant something else, tell me and I'll undo it") and the owner did not correct it before this entry was written; unratified. If the owner meant otherwise, this entry is to be superseded on contact.]

**What the answer does.** (1) A provision whose question is whether it is present — a hearing loop, a visual alarm, a tactile walking surface indicator, a TTY, tactile signage and the like — is **not a parameter**, and **no value is computed for it**: OR-14b holds that the element is never a container figures are filed into (`grep -n -F 'The element is still never a container that figures are filed into' references/project-standards.md`), and OD-24 holds that a parameter is one measured quantity with a kind (`grep -n -F 'OD-24: flat and coarse' references/project-standards.md`). (2) The element-level determination that T-060 builds points at the **provision terms** the element should carry (`terms.term_id`), together with the **source statements** that say it should (observation rows, `observed_terms.observation_id`, or extraction rows, `source_value_extractions.extraction_id`), as well as at its parameters' determinations (`specifications.specification_id`) and at the relation rows that cross them, as T-060's design constraint already requires; it holds **no value column**. The provision gets its mark only through that pointer, on the element's page. (3) It overtakes OD-53's allowance to decline such a provision term as "not a measurement" **only in this sense**: the provision term is kept and pointed at by the element determination instead of being left unreferenced. A declination of such a term *as a parameter* stays lawful under OD-53 — under option (b) the term is not a parameter; it is a provision reached by pointer — but a declination that would leave the term with no pointer possible is not made. (4) Options (a), (c) and (d) are not taken: no quantity kind of presence is added, no separate register of provisions is built, and prose alone is not the mechanism. (5) OD-56 needs no delegated resolution and no independent check; the delegated route registered in the thirteenth set (its ACTION (4)) is not run.

**What the answer does not decide.** (1) How a source's statement "a hearing loop should be provided" is typed — a relation between the observation and the element term, or a term-to-term pointer with the observation as its warrant: that is for T-060's design file (`workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/tasks/T-060-design.md`, not yet written) and the pointer kinds of T-029 (`grep -n -i 'kind' workplan/2026-11-12-bidirectional-pipeline/tasks/T-029-term-relations.md`). (2) Where a provision that belongs to no one element is reached (a TTY at a service counter, a building's alarm system), which the register entry's option (b) named as its hard case: T-060's design names the element it hangs from or reports that it has none. (3) What mark a provision carries on the element's page, and whether ●, ◐ and ○ read for it as they do for a value: render's (T-037, after T-060), from the evidence of the source statements pointed at. (4) Which held terms are provisions of presence: judged term by term by the session that writes the pointer, never by a list maintained beside the terms (`CLAUDE.md` rule 8).

**The earlier records this answer affects, none of them edited.** The thirteenth set's statement that OD-56 is open for a delegated resolution (its answer 4 and ACTION (4)) is overtaken: OD-56 is answered by the owner. OD-53's allowance is read as in (3) above; OD-53 keeps its status line. The register entry's options and their consequences stay as they were put.

CONDITION: any session designing or building the element-level determination (T-060) or the typed pointers between terms (T-029); declining, deferring or reviewing a held term that names a provision whose question is presence (T-072 item 6, T-087, or any research batch); or deciding how such a provision appears in the book.
ACTION: (1) T-060 carries the answer: its element determination row also points at the provision terms the element should carry and at the source statements (observations or extraction rows) that say it should, still with no value column, and its design names the test that the table has none. (2) T-029 carries the pointer kind: its CHECK includes an element-to-provision kind (`element has provision`) with a test that it is accepted and that an undeclared kind is refused; it is the carrier T-060 reads. (3) T-072 item 6 and T-087 treat a provision term as reached by pointer: such a term is not declined without that pointer being possible ("a provision of presence is reached by an element pointer (OD-56), not declined away"). (4) Read OD-56 through the gate; it needs no delegated resolution and no check line. (5) Treat the [ASSUMPTION] above as unratified.

**On convergence (statements 1 to 3).** [ASSUMPTION: the owner's meaning of convergence is descriptive: values coming together, whatever the sources, as in the thirteenth set's answer 1 ("convergence is possible too, and it helps define most common practice (which isn't same as best practice)") — basis: "convergence just means values coming together", and "why wouldn't that make sense" answers a report that the word had been withheld from agreement among codes; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: the written definition of `convergent` in `governance/evidence-methodology.md` §3.2 joins two facts in one word: whether values come together (descriptive), and whether the agreement is corroboration by independent evidence (a confidence claim: "The converged claim carries higher confidence than either dimension alone", against the doctrine `grep -n -F 'precedent counts documents; evidence counts roots' governance/evidence-architecture.md`, under which agreeing codes are one fact about transmission, not many facts). tests 1 and 2 of `skills/run-pipeline_SKILL.md` §6), and convergence only describes where values cluster, which is most common practice — basis: "convergence is not evidence of best practice but most common practice", which repeats the owner's words of 2026-11-06 (`grep -n -F 'is NOT evidence of best practice but most common practice' references/project-standards.md`); Two sources can differ because one targets everyone and the other a named group; that is a reason to read the clusters apart, not a defect in either — basis: the statement names exactly these two and calls them reasons for divergence; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: "etc" leaves the list open: statements 5 and 7 together are a starting list the investigation prints, not a closed vocabulary, and a divergence that no listed check separates is reported as "undetermined", which is itself the cue for a human read — basis: "etc", and CLAUDE.md rule 8 (no curated list beside the typed columns); unratified.]

**On ranges (statements 8 and 9).** [ASSUMPTION: "it" is the convergence and divergence discussion: a range can itself be the value a source states, and two point values that look divergent (1000 mm and 1200 mm) may be the two ends of one permitted range another source states (a mirror mounted 1000 to 1200 mm above finished floor) — basis: the example names exactly such a range, and the statement followed the list of causes; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: "we need to include ranges" makes the range the general case and the point its special case: cluster membership is defined over the interval each figure states, not over bare point values. The intervals are already derived, from `claimed_value`, `claimed_unit`, `comparator` and `claim_type`, by `grep -n -F 'def bound_on_scale' scripts/dbcore.py`: a point is (v, v); a floor ("at least 1200") is (v, open) for `>=` and `>`; a ceiling ("no more than 1200") is (open, v) for `<=` and `<`; a range or `between` written "a to b" is (a, b); strictness (`>` against `>=`) is deliberately not modelled. No bound column is missing — basis: the follow-up statement, and the function that already exists; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: a range is never reduced to a point or its midpoint for grouping; both bounds are kept, as statement 6's reading keeps stored values exact — basis: statement 6's reading, and "include ranges"; unratified.] [ASSUMPTION: two relations over intervals are needed and are kept apart. **Consistent:** two intervals overlap, or come within the tolerance of overlapping (a point inside a range is consistent with it; two overlapping ranges are consistent; a floor and a ceiling are consistent where the floor is not above the ceiling) — the fact OR-6b calls claims that can all hold. **The same:** each finite bound within the tolerance of the other's, with the same sides open — values coming together. The recorder's working reading groups clusters by **the same**, and reports **consistent** beside it (a range that spans two clusters is printed as spanning them), because grouping by overlap would make every floor converge with every higher floor and a broad range converge with everything inside it, so that "convergent" would say only "can all hold", which OR-6b already names, and 1000 mm and 1200 mm would merge through the 1000 to 1200 mm range that statement 8 asks to see as spanning them. The alternative (group by consistency) is the owner's to choose; the question put is that choice only — basis: the two relations are different facts, and statement 8's example needs the second reported, not merged; unratified.]
